To test the best solar gutter lights, we installed each light and took note of how easy or difficult the assembly and installation process was. We recorded the charging time for each and whether the lights performed well on sunny days, cloudy days and after a rainstorm. We noted how bright the lights were and if there were any additional features, such as motion detection or the ability to pivot or adjust the lights. The lights were left up for 2 weeks, including during a heavy summer rainstorm.

Each tested solar gutter light was evaluated on a scale of 1 to 5 across multiple categories: setup, appearance, light quality, durability, and value. We compiled the totals to get the rating for each category and ranked the solar gutter lights accordingly.

Before shopping for the best solar lights, there are some points to consider. Below are some of the factors that go into choosing the best solar gutter lights to help you decide which type is best for your specific application.

Note: These kinds of lights run independently of solar roof lights, a solar power system, or solar generator, so they don’t require any hardwiring or complicated installation.

Similar to solar garden path lights, the best solar gutter lights come in a wide range of brightness levels. Your lighting needs will determine which product is best for you. Many small clip-on lights offer just enough accent lighting to add a tasteful glow to your property. These lights provide between 20 and 50 lumens each, which is enough to illuminate a garden bed or a bit of a walkway relatively well.

For more light, there are models available with outputs over 500 lumens. These lights are excellent for lighting a driveway, fence line, or other areas you’d prefer to keep brightly lit.

Since most solar gutter lights attach directly to a home’s gutters, the materials chosen are an important factor. A light made with bulky materials, such as heavy-duty metal, will eventually bend and deform a gutter, so heft is worth considering.

By and large, the best solar gutter lights are made of plastic. These lights are lightweight but durable enough to withstand the elements. However, the brackets used to clip them to the gutter are usually steel, which gives these units the strength they need to withstand wind and heavy rains. Some of the lights that attach directly to the home’s structure might be aluminum, which is a more corrosion-resistant material than steel, and it’s also lighter weight.

Another important consideration is the IP rating. An IP rating (or Ingress Protection rating) indicates how well a light can stand up to elements like dust and water. The first number indicates how dustproof a light is on a scale of 1 to 5. The second number is water resistance. A number between 1 and 6 indicates a product is water-resistant, and 7 or 8 means it’s waterproof. Most of the lights on this list are either a 5 or 6 for being dustproof, and although they are all water-resistant, none are waterproof. Because these lights are charged by the rays of the sun, keeping them dust-free and keeping your gutters clean will directly influence the effectiveness of these lights.

It’s also important to note that while many of the lights claim to work at least 8 hours after a day of charging, cold weather can affect how well they stay charged. Therefore, these lights may perform for shorter periods, closer to 6 hours, when used during the winter or in cooler climates.

Most of the best solar gutter lights will automatically turn on when their built-in photosensors recognize that it’s getting dark. The length of time they run for, or their operating time, will vary depending on battery size, light output, and how much sun each light receives during a given day. Most of the lights we tested required 6 to 8 hours of charging time to last an average of 8 hours in the night, with some able to run as long as 10 to 12 hours.

Solar gutter lights have onboard batteries that charge using energy from the sun, similar to those found on solar chargers. The light absorbs solar energy via a built-in photovoltaic cell. The cell then converts the energy into electricity and stores it in the battery. For some of the lights, the battery is a built-in component, but others take a rechargeable AA battery. This battery is most often included with the lights and may need to be replaced every 12 months to 3 years, depending on manufacturer’s recommendation and how much weather the lights have endured.

Battery power output can vary between 800 and up to 3,000 milliamp hours, and the larger the battery, the more energy it can produce. The panel’s size relative to the battery’s size determines how efficiently the light will charge. Too large a cell and the battery will fill too quickly, wasting sunlight. Too small and a battery may never reach its full potential. For this reason, most manufacturers try to size their photovoltaic cells to match their output ratings.

Choosing the best solar gutter lights for your home might come down to ease of installation. Many of these models simply attach to the lip of a home’s gutters, tightening down onto the edge. All that’s required to secure them is a ladder and a pair of pliers or a screwdriver, depending on the model.

Other models might require the user to attach them directly to the home’s structure with brackets and screws. While this could seem like an inconvenience, these models typically have the greatest light output and battery life, so there is a trade-off. Installing these lights typically requires a ladder, a power drill, and a screwdriver.

In general, most solar lights are function first, meaning they may not add to a home’s curb appeal. But, there are models available that aren’t as jarring to the eye. For the most part, gutters on homes are white, so most gutter lights are available in white plastic. Homes with gutters in other colors might look better with black solar gutter lights, which are readily available.

If you’re unable to find a light that matches well with your home’s aesthetic, you can still achieve attractive outdoor lighting by finding a model that attaches directly to the home’s structure. This allows them to be hidden under the eave somewhat, but it’s important that they get at least some sunlight to function as expected.

Some additional features help increase the convenience and functionality of the best solar gutter lights. For example, a motion sensor might activate when it senses someone or something walking underneath it, which is excellent for security.

Many models also come with automatic switches that activate the light once a sensor determines it’s getting dark. And should the battery still have enough charge by morning, the controller will turn the light off when the sun comes up. There are also models with remote controls, which allow you to operate the lights remotely and adjust their output without climbing a ladder.

If you still have some questions about the best solar gutter lights, this section is for you. It’s a collection of some of the most frequently asked questions about solar gutter lights. Be sure to check for an answer to your question below.

Solar lights work best when the light is most available. While solar gutter lights are weatherproof and designed for year-round use, it’s important to remember that heavy snowfall can break and damage gutters, and also the lights.

Solar gutter lights typically come with everything necessary for setup and use.

There are solar lights that produce as much as 1,500 lumens of brightness.

Most solar lights are not motion activated, but there are solar lights that have motion-activated features.

If properly cared for, many solar gutter lights can last for years. Manufacturers frequently offer a 1-year warranty on gutter lights, but this can vary depending on the product, material, and type of weather the lights may endure.

This armored amphibian once lived and hunted on the seafloor

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Every print subscription comes with full digital access

The ancient creature differs from all other known amphibians in its ability to tolerate saltwater

A Gerrothorax maritimus fossil unearthed in China was discovered in an ancient seabed, suggesting it's the first and only amphibian known to have lived in the ocean.

Most amphibians shrivel or suffocate in saltwater. But long ago, an oddball armored amphibian may have dwelled deep in the ocean.

Scientists have found evidence that an ancient amphibian with a flattened body and upturned eyes may have once stalked the seafloor. The animal, Gerrothorax maritimus, is the first ever amphibian known to have lived without access to land or freshwater, researchers report October 26 in Nature Ecology & Evolution.

G. maritimus, which lived 237 million years ago, was rather unlike today’s amphibians, which breathe and drink through their porous skin. The creature’s lifestyle was unimaginable for an amphibian, says Ryosuke Motani, a paleobiologist at the University of California, Davis. Of the more than 8,000 amphibians known today, none live solely in saltwater.

We summarize the week's scientific breakthroughs every Thursday.

Motani and his colleagues inspected two well-preserved G. maritimus fossils from the late Triassic Period, which spanned roughly 237 million to 201 million years ago. Each creature measured around 70 centimeters, about as long as a skateboard, and was swathed in armor made of bony plates. The specimens appeared nearly identical to their closest relatives, other amphibians that dwelled in freshwater, Motani says. But the fossils’ location was unusual: They were found in what was once a seabed, roughly 30 kilometers from any ancient land.

It wasn’t clear whether the creatures lived in the ocean or were landlubbers that got buried there after they perished. So the team performed a chemical analysis. Levels of different forms of strontium in the fossil bones matched those of the Triassic ocean. This telltale sign revealed that G. maritimus ate seafood and drank seawater. “That means it lived there,” Motani says.

For amphibians, “this is the first time that that we have hard evidence that they were actually adapted for long-term life in the sea,” says Lachlan Hart, a paleobiologist at the University of New South Wales in Sydney who was not involved with the new study. Hart and his colleagues previously suggested that a different ancient amphibian may have been seafaring but probably did not dwell in the ocean.

Scientists have long searched for a marine amphibian. The fossil record is littered with skeletal fragments revealing how some mammals, reptiles and birds have adapted to a salty lifestyle. “Amphibians were always the problem because they did not really follow the rule,” Motani says.

The same skin that makes today’s amphibians vulnerable to disease and toxins also makes saltwater fearsome. Key adaptations allowed G. maritimus to skirt these issues. G. maritimus and its relatives evolved bony body armor, probably for defense. But these hard plates would have helped the seafloor dwellers prevent water loss. And unlike modern amphibians, which typically lose their internal gills after the larval stage, this creature and its close relatives retained a fishy breathing strategy suitable for life in the depths.

G. maritimus fossils hint that the animal lurked on the seafloor as an ambush predator. Its jaw lifted upward like a toilet seat so that it could unleash a suction attack on fish and other small creatures.

There may yet be other amphibians hiding in ancient oceans, environments ignored by paleontologists studying these creatures. “Because they’re amphibians, we would be looking for them in terrestrial freshwater environments,” Hart says. Perhaps there are more amphibians that have gotten their sea legs.

Questions or comments on this article? E-mail us at feedback@sciencenews.org | Reprints FAQ

R. Motani et al. A fully marine stem amphibian from the Late Triassic of China. Nature Ecology & Evolution. 2026. doi: 10.1038/s41559-026-03209-9

B.P. Kear et al. Revision of the trematosaurid Erythrobatrachus noonkanbahensis confirms a cryptic marine temnospondyl community from the Lower Triassic of Western Australia. Journal of Vertebrate Paleontology. 2026. doi: 10.1080/02724634.2025.2601224

Argentine Bitcoiners Debut “The Genesis of Satoshi Nakamoto” Artwork in Downtown Buenos Aires

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

“The Genesis of Satoshi Nakamoto,” a 2.4-meter, multi-dimensional cube, was presented on October 29 at Polo Dot as part of the Open Fest of LABITCONF 2026. Driven by Bitcoin.ar through the Premio B·Arte, the work combines art, technology, and education in a journey through Bitcoin’s fundamental principles. The inauguration featured guided remarks by Rodolfo Andragnes and a musical performance by Mayumaná, according to an announcement by the organization.

Buenos Aires now has a monument dedicated to Satoshi Nakamoto, the person—or group of people—behind the creation of Bitcoin, whose identity remains unknown.

Driven by Bitcoin.ar through the fifth edition of the Premio B·Arte, the monument was conceived not only as a tribute to Satoshi, but also as an artistic and educational experience. Through its five faces—four lateral and one upper—it proposes a journey to approach different concepts that explain Bitcoin’s origin, functioning, and philosophy.

During the inauguration, Rodolfo Andragnes — co-founder of Bitcoin.ar and LABITCONF — accompanied the public with words and explanations about the details of the work, guiding the reading of its various elements and the meaning behind each representation. The presentation was accompanied by a musical show by Mayumaná, which added rhythm and sound to the opening of this new space dedicated to Bitcoin’s history.

Each of the monument’s faces offers a gateway to a different aspect of Bitcoin. The figure of Satoshi Nakamoto, for example, represents the anonymous character of its creator and the collective dimension of a technology that, once set in motion, continued to grow thanks to thousands of people distributed around the world. The reflections and multiplied geometric forms also evoke the structure of a network that replicates and expands.

Another of its faces addresses Bitcoin’s programmed scarcity through a cube that successively divides into halves in a Fibonacci sequence. The representation refers to “the halving”, the mechanism that approximately every four years halves the reward miners receive for validating blocks and that regulates the issuance of new bitcoins. Starting at 50 bitcoins per block, the total reward gets cut in half every four years for roughly 130 years, until 21 million bitcoins have been emitted and the block reward becomes zero. This supply limit is iconic to the cryptocurrency and key to its qualities as hard money.

The work also incorporates the first words of Bitcoin’s foundational document, the white paper published by Satoshi Nakamoto in October 31st,  2008. There, the possibility of making electronic payments directly between people, without needing to go through a financial institution, is proposed. That idea constitutes a foundational principle of the technology.

Another face reproduces information from the genesis block, the first block of the Bitcoin chain, created on January 3, 2009. Among its elements appears the reference to the headline from The Times newspaper that Satoshi incorporated into that block, a historical mark that places Bitcoin’s birth in its context and anchors it to history and time.

Finally, on the upper part are two expressions that synthesize principles associated with this technology: “Don’t trust, verify”, an invitation to check information for oneself, rather than trust at face value. And “Vires in numeris,” Latin for “strength in numbers,” which refers to the safety of the crowd when it comes to privacy, as well as the self-evident, self-asserting strength of a money built and defended by cryptography rather than authority or military might.

Thus, the monument proposes something more than contemplating a sculpture: it invites one to walk around it, observe it from different angles, and discover the concepts behind each of its parts; “The intention is to bring Bitcoin closer to those who are already part of its community, but also to awaken the curiosity of those who do not yet know its history.”

The monument was created by Ricardo Alonso, engineer, and Celeste Difabio Videla, architect, a couple of artists from Bariloche who won the fifth edition of the Premio B·Arte. The work measures 2.40 meters in width, length, and height, and is made of stainless steel and glass.

Its construction involved teams and collaborators from different localities across the country, in a process that combined different trades, materials, and techniques. Aída Pippo, visual artist, researcher of new technologies applied to art, and member of the Board of Directors of Bitcoin.ar, led the project and coordinated the different stages of production and assembly.

“It gives me great pride to feel that this is something we can leave in our city and in our country,” Andragnes expressed during the presentation, highlighting the value of a collective initiative that seeks to leave a cultural and educational footprint.

For their part, the creators noted that they sought for the work to function as a meeting point for the community and, at the same time, as a gateway for those who do not yet know Bitcoin. “You don’t always have the possibility of leaving something for posterity,” they shared when speaking of the experience of seeing the monument finished and installed in Buenos Aires.

With this inauguration, Bitcoin.ar seeks for the monument to become a permanent resource for the city: a space that schools, universities, families, and visitors can approach to learn Bitcoin’s history, discover its principles, and open new conversations about the future of money and technology. The intention is that what is presented today as an artistic work can continue to grow through educational activities and new experiences, and remain in Buenos Aires as a legacy for future generations.

The project was financed by the Fundación Bitcoin Iberoamérica and represents a new way of bringing Bitcoin’s history and principles to public and cultural spaces, beyond screens and specialized environments.

Library by Collective Architecture breathes new life into Scottish high street

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Film and photography by Jim Stephenson

The conversion of a dilapidated high-street building into a public library in Paisley, Scotland, shortlisted for the RIAS Andrew Doolan Best Building in Scotland Award, features in this video by photographer Jim Stephenson.

The film forms part of a series being published by Dezeen in collaboration with the Royal Incorporation of Architects in Scotland (RIAS) to highlight the annual award’s 2026 shortlist.

In the video, viewers are introduced to Paisley Central Library, designed by Scottish studio Collective Architecture to serve as a welcoming community space with reading areas, event spaces and a study room.

According to studio director Ewan Imrie, Paisley Central Library was conceived as a civic space for “people to dwell”.

“The client was very clear from the outset that the building had to be as welcoming to all different types of people from all aspects of society,” Imrie said.

“Encouraging people to dwell was the most important thing and if they pick up a book and read it then that’s a bonus.”

Established in 2002, the Andrew Doolan Best Building in Scotland Award is an annual award that highlights Scottish projects, with this year’s winner set to be announced on 26 November.

Comprising three reuse projects, RIAS said this year’s shortlisted projects tap into “important conversations around community, collaboration, retrofit and reuse”. Shortlisted alongside the Paisley Central Library is the Edinburgh Futures Institute by Bennetts Associates and the Tarlair Outdoor Pool Pavilion by Studio Octopi.

Last year’s winner was a public park renewed by studios Stallan-Brand and LDA Design.

The film and photography is courtesy of Jim Stephenson.

The Sky Today on Friday, November 6: Nab the North America Nebula

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Looking for a sky event this week? Check out our full Sky This Week column.

As fall gets into full swing, we are seeing less of the summertime stars. By 6 P.M. local time, the Summer Triangle — which flies high overhead on short summer nights — is now starting to set in the west. Lowest of its three points is Vega in Lyra; to Vega’s upper left is Altair in Aquila, while Deneb in Cygnus is almost directly above Vega and the highest of the three.

With no Moon in the sky, let’s use Deneb to find a favorite deep-sky object: NGC 7000, the North America Nebula. It’s a great small scope target, and its location just 3° east of Deneb makes it easy to center in the eyepiece. However, this huge emission nebula stretches about 2° across, so note that it requires a larger field of view to capture all in one go. It definitely benefits from a nebula filter if you have one.

On the sky, the nebula’s western side looks like the eastern coast of the continent for which it is named, and vice versa. The curve that mimics the Gulf of Mexico is particularly prominent, so start there if you’re having trouble seeing the shape. Look also to NGC 7000’s west — across a dark gulf, you’ll see a fainter, more compact patch of nebulosity. This is IC 5067/5070, also known as the Pelican Nebula.

Sunrise: 6:35 A.M.Sunset: 4:52 P.M.Moonrise: 3:56 A.M.Moonset: 3:16 P.M.Moon Phase: Waning crescent (5%)*Times for sunrise, sunset, moonrise, and moonset are given in local time from 40° N 90° W. The Moon’s illumination is given at 10 P.M. local time from the same location.

Alison Klesman is senior editor of Astronomy magazine. She holds a Ph.D. in astronomy and has studied a variety of topics, from minor planets to supermassive black holes.

Bitcoin Tumbles to $75K as AI Fears Wipe $150B From Crypto

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Bitcoin Tumbles to $75K as AI Fears Wipe $150B From Crypto

Massive liquidations shake digital assets as growing fears over AI spending ripple through global markets.

Bitcoin fell to $75,350 on November 6 as a broader crypto market sell-off wiped nearly $150 billion from the digital asset market in 24 hours.

The sharp downturn came as crypto assets tracked a broader retreat from risk assets, sparked by concerns over artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure spending that triggered a major Wall Street sell-off.

According to CoinGecko, total crypto market capitalization declined by more than 5.1%, while leveraged traders faced $1.25 billion in liquidations, piling further pressure onto already falling prices.

Ethereum fell 7.9%, XRP dropped 8.7% and Solana declined 6.4% over the same period, per CoinGecko data. The crypto market sell-off followed a sharp decline in US technology stocks on Thursday.

The November 5 rout wiped roughly $2.5 trillion from US stock market value. The Nasdaq Composite plunged 5.52%, marking its worst trading session since April 2025 and pushing the index more than 10% below its October 6 record high.

The S&P 500 and the Dow also posted their worst sessions since then.

Investor concerns grew that Democratic gains in the US midterms could slow AI infrastructure expansion, particularly in Texas. Governor-elect Gina Hinojosa proposed pausing new data center construction until stronger protections are introduced, raising uncertainty over major investments by Google, Meta, Oracle and OpenAI.

AI and semiconductor stocks weakened ahead of Thursday’s broader sell-off. On Friday, November 6, South Korea’s Kospi plunged nearly 9% at the open, triggering a 20-minute trading halt.

Nevertheless, some experts suggest that the AI stock sell-off could ultimately benefit the health of the market.

Ed Yardeni, president of Yardeni Research, told CNN that slowing AI infrastructure investment could help cool an increasingly speculative market.

“We want a slowdown in the AI buildout. It was too frenzied and speculative,” Yardeni said, adding that allowing air to escape the AI bubble gradually would be preferable to an abrupt collapse.

Concerns about excessive data center construction have also intensified as investors question whether future demand will justify the industry’s aggressive infrastructure spending.

Meanwhile, demand for government bonds increased during the sell-off, pushing the 10-year US Treasury yield down to 4.84%, its lowest level in two months.

Bitcoin and altcoins are trading in step with tech stocks, so equity-market shocks can quickly hit crypto prices. Heavy long liquidations show how leverage can amplify downside moves.

XRP sellers face massive slip as USD order book depth fakes size

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

At the Nov. 4 close, a capped sample of Bitstamp-issued USD offers held less funded inventory than the smaller RLUSD book.

An XRP Ledger order book sample advertised 1.28 million Bitstamp-issued USD tokens at the Nov. 4 close, but the offer owners' balances funded only about 57,000.

For an XRP holder selling into those offers, the headline quantity overstated the token inventory available to trade.

The smaller sample for dollar stablecoin Ripple USD (RLUSD) held about 92,000 funded tokens, with no gap between quoted and funded amounts at that ledger.

A Nov. 5 refresh, at ledger 108132129, closed at 13:09 UTC, still showed a large Bitstamp-dollar gap of roughly 1.31 million tokens quoted against 58,179 funded. RLUSD had about 80,435 quoted and 75,726 funded, so its earlier fully funded reading had not persisted.

The Nov. 4 reading covered validated ledger 108120236, closed at 23:59:51 UTC. Bitstamp-issued USD offers totaled 1,280,737 tokens quoted and 56,887 funded, a funded share of 4.44%.

One offer advertised 1,241,760 USD tokens while its funded amount was only 17,967. That single offer accounted for almost the entire difference, showing why adding advertised order sizes can mislead about what a counterparty can deliver.

The measurement requests at most 100 offers per side and counts quantities within 10% of each book's own reference midpoint, derived from its tightest uncrossed bid and ask. NOSHASHI, the ledger-data service, calls the dollar-token side that XRP sellers receive the “ask.”

XRPL's offer rules allow an owner to advertise more than it currently holds because placing an offer does not lock its full amount. The official book offers interface normally omits unfunded offers and identifies partially funded amounts, so software can distinguish advertised size from supported size.

An oversized Bitstamp USD offer drove the November 4 gap. Compare both book samples at the next day's measured ledger.

At the November 4 close, the smaller RLUSD sample had more funded inventory than the much larger quoted Bitstamp-dollar sample.

At the refreshed November 5 ledger, RLUSD was partly funded too, but its sampled funded inventory still exceeded Bitstamp USD.

A larger advertised book can contain less funded token inventory for XRP sellers.

Different issuer-token units, not interchangeable bank dollars. Capped samples do not measure all XRP liquidity or guarantee near-best-price execution. AMM reserves are excluded. The single-offer diagram is November 4 only; the date selector changes the complete two-book comparison below.

RLUSD's user terms name Standard Custody & Trust Company LLC as issuer, so direct redemption with Ripple requires being an eligible Customer under a separate agreement. Receiving either token on-ledger differs from receiving spendable dollars in a bank account.

Using the Nov. 5 ledger, a static model swept funded offers in price order for a 10,000-XRP sale. A separate model used each automated market maker (AMM), which trades against pool reserves, applying its ordinary trading fee in a constant-product calculation.

The book estimates use the 100-offer cap and 10% band. Pool estimates include ordinary AMM trading fees, and all four exclude additional issuer-transfer, network, and redemption costs, as well as wallet and trust-line constraints.

In the same model, a 50,000-XRP sale exhausted the sampled Bitstamp-dollar offers after about 47,484 XRP, returning 58,179 USD tokens and leaving 2,516 XRP unsold. The sampled RLUSD offers absorbed the modeled 50,000 XRP for about 64,960 RLUSD.

The RLUSD AMM held about 1.83 million XRP and 2.39 million RLUSD, versus only about 3,489 XRP and 4,594 USD tokens in the Bitstamp-dollar pool.

Quoted size, funded inventory, and completed trading volume answer different questions. For a seller, the useful test is a refreshed estimate for the intended size and acceptable issuer token, including the costs and redemption access needed to turn that token into cash.

Hydroxyapatite toothpaste promises to better protect your teeth. Here’s what the science says.

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 6, 2026 8:49 AM EST

There’s a new kid on the block in the toothpaste aisle. Next to the usual fluoride formulas, another option has been appearing: hydroxyapatite toothpaste.

Hydroxyapatite is the same mineral found in tooth enamel, and some claim using hydroxyapatite toothpaste can help plug tiny cracks in your teeth. Many brands are touting the new toothpaste as an effective alternative to fluoride. But does it actually protect your teeth, or is it just another trendy wellness product? After speaking with several experts, it seems like hydroxyapatite toothpaste might be worth trying.

Hydroxyapatite is a mineral made mostly of calcium and phosphorus that naturally occurs in human bones and teeth. It’s the main mineral in tooth enamel, the hard outer coating that helps protect your teeth from damage.

“Enamel is the hardest substance in the human body, but it’s not invulnerable,” says Dr. Joachim Enax, a chemist and senior scientist at Dr. Kurt Wolff GmbH & Co. KG, which makes hydroxyapatite toothpastes. Acids from foods and from bacteria in the mouth can gradually dissolve minerals from the tooth surface. Toothpastes that contain hydroxyapatite are designed to put some of that mineral back and help repair very early enamel damage, Enax explains.

If you’ve looked at these products, you may have seen labels mentioning “nano-hydroxyapatite” or “micro-hydroxyapatite.” That refers to the size of the mineral particles in the toothpaste. “Nano crystals are very fine and microscopic. Micro-hydroxyapatite crystals are larger,” says Dr. Hardy Limeback, a professor emeritus in the faculty of dentistry at the University of Toronto, who has been critical of fluoride use.

Both nano- and micro-hydroxyapatite occur naturally—for example, in breast milk—and because they are chemically the same as the mineral in tooth enamel, “they are added to toothpaste to plug up micro cracks or etches on the tooth surface,” he explains.

Hydroxyapatite toothpaste is not actually new. In the 1960s, research at NASA on growing crystals for electronics led to a patent for repairing teeth by growing hydroxyapatite crystals. A Japanese company later acquired the patent and introduced the first hydroxyapatite-based toothpastes in the 1980s.

Since hydroxyapatite was approved as an anti-cavity ingredient in Japan in 1993, similar toothpastes have become available in Europe (since 2006) and Canada (since 2015).

In the United States, you can buy hydroxyapatite toothpastes online and in some stores, but neither the Food and Drug Administration nor the American Dental Association has approved hydroxyapatite as an anti-cavity ingredient. To earn the ADA’s Seal of Acceptance—an official indication that a toothpaste helps prevent cavities—a product must contain fluoride.

Hydroxyapatite toothpaste can help remineralize enamel that has been weakened by acids from dental plaque or acidic foods, say Limeback and Enax. By promoting remineralization and forming a protective layer that helps shield enamel from acids, it may also protect teeth against cavities, says Dr. Elżbieta Paszyńska, a dental surgeon and head of the department of integrated dentistry and endodontics at Poznan University of Medical Sciences in Poland.

Fluoride prevents cavities in a similar way: Fluoride ions are incorporated into the enamel’s structure, helping repair very early decay and making the enamel more resistant to acid over time.

Paszyńska’s group ran two randomized clinical trials at two medical universities, with nearly 200 participants each: one in children aged three to seven, and one in adults with healthy teeth. For 12 to 18 months, participants used either a hydroxyapatite or a fluoride toothpaste. (Both studies were funded by Dr. Kurt Wolff GmbH & Co. KG.)

“At the end of both studies, children and adults using the hydroxyapatite toothpaste were no more likely to develop new carious lesions than those using fluoride toothpaste,” she says. “These findings demonstrated, in two independent clinical trials, that hydroxyapatite toothpaste can provide caries [cavity] protection comparable to fluoride toothpaste in daily oral care.”

Hydroxyapatite toothpastes may also help reduce tooth sensitivity by physically filling and sealing exposed dentinal tubules (the tiny channels leading to tooth nerves), Limeback and Enax note. In one study, people who used a nano-hydroxyapatite toothpaste for two to four weeks were less sensitive to cold air and touch than they had been at the start.

Early studies—most conducted in laboratory settings—suggest that regular use of hydroxyapatite toothpastes can also have a whitening effect. The mineral can form a thin, white layer on the tooth surface that helps mask the yellow tones of the underlying dentin and reduce the appearance of stains.

“This is different from bleaching; it is about restoring and maintaining the natural appearance of enamel,” Enax says. But more clinical trials are needed to confirm this effect in people.

Why our ancestors had straight teeth without braces

Is crossing your eyes really bad for you? We asked an optometrist.

In Europe, health officials have closely studied nano-hydroxyapatite in toothpastes and mouthwashes. In July 2025, the Scientific Committee on Consumer Safety said this ingredient is safe in amounts up to about 30 percent in toothpaste and 10 percent in mouthwash. Most products on the market use 10 percent or less, which is well within that limit.

In the studies they reviewed, rod-shaped nano-hydroxyapatite did not damage DNA or harm cells, even when tested at high concentrations on mouth tissue samples in the lab. (The safety opinion does not apply to products that use needle-shaped particles.)

The committee concluded that any tiny amount of nano-hydroxyapatite particles that enter the cells lining the mouth is naturally removed from the body when those cells shed. If you swallow some toothpaste by accident, the particles are expected to break down quickly in stomach acid, causing no harmful effects.

Even with growing evidence in its favor, some U.S. dentists are cautious about swapping fluoride for hydroxyapatite.

“As far as telling someone, ‘Hey, stop using fluoride toothpaste and start using this,’ we don’t have enough evidence yet,” Dr. Hema Arany, a dentist and associate professor at the Medical University of South Carolina, said in a recent interview. “And so when it comes to standard of care, we are not recommending hydroxyapatite toothpaste.”

But some people avoid fluoride because they’re worried about possible links between fluoride and skin problems or neurodevelopmental issues in children, though these concerns remain debated and the data is still evolving. The World Health Organization and the American Dental Association maintain that fluoride toothpaste is safe and highly effective at preventing tooth decay when used as directed.

According to ethical guidance published in the Journal of the American Dental Association, for people who refuse to use fluoride, hydroxyapatite “is likely the second-best option.”

lavazza tablì wins designboom special prize third place at seoul design award

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

lavazza's tablì tabs eliminates capsules from the single-cup experience.

Sustainability flourishes in simplicity. Caring for the planet becomes easy when real, convenient and functional alternatives replace more polluting options. Lavazza kept this reality in mind with Tablì, a capsule-free single-serve coffee system that has earned third place in the designboom Special Prize at the Seoul Design Award 2026.

Until now, coffee drinkers have had three main options: go through the hassle of preparing a pot each time they wanted to enjoy the drink, use instant coffee or turn to a capsule machine. The third option is widespread because of its convenience, but there is a hidden side that not everyone is aware of.

According to figures from Deutsche Umwelthilfe, cited by Lavazza in its award submission, around 20 billion coffee capsules are used, and therefore discarded, every year. Germany alone consumes around 3.4 billion, generating approximately 13,500 metric tons of waste annually.

Hailing from Italy, a country where coffee is both a rule and a tradition, the multidisciplinary team, led by Florian Seidl and joined by Angelica Rella, Marco Rotundo and Nicola Bettoni, was well aware of this problem.

The team posed a revolutionary question: what if there were another way to prepare coffee? Tablì was born from the ambition to replicate the convenience of capsule coffee while designing a product with minimal environmental impact.

Their vision went beyond replacing capsules with a less harmful form of packaging. Instead, they designed a system built around a tab made entirely of coffee. No plastic, aluminum, additives or coatings. Alongside the tabs, they completed the ecosystem by designing a compatible machine, packaging, accessories and a connectivity system.

Lavazza Tablì considers environmental care from production through maintenance and everyday use. The machine is made using post-consumer plastics, and its construction takes repairability and low energy consumption into account. Moreover, because the tabs are made entirely of coffee, they generate only organic waste.

This commitment to sustainability has been key to securing the Special Prize with designboom. The Seoul Design Award’s purpose is precisely to promote projects that have achieved harmony between people, society and the environment.

The designboom Special Prize introduces a series of additional requirements. Award-winning projects must connect with designboom’s values: Intentionally Curious, Disrupt Playfully, and Fearlessly Meaningful. Ultimately, they must demonstrate an ability to think outside the box, create products that bring us joy and pursue design that, beyond being beautiful, expresses a bold vision with functionality rooted in its very conception.

Lavazza Tablì has the honor of taking third place in the category for its attention to detail, aesthetics and functionality as a whole rather than as separate elements. The product’s specifications speak for themselves: the height-adjustable cup rest with a metal grid; the sliding opening for inserting the tab; and the transparent water tank that allows users to check the water level are examples of a project conceived for intuitive, smooth operation that elevates the user experience while protecting the environment.

Kalshi hit with injunction over sports contracts on California tribal lands

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

A federal judge has granted a preliminary injunction barring Kalshi from offering sports-event contracts on the lands of Blue Lake Rancheria and the Chicken Ranch Rancheria of Me-Wuk Indians.

A federal judge has granted a preliminary injunction barring Kalshi from offering sports-event contracts on the lands of two California tribes.

In a Wednesday order, U.S. District Judge Jacqueline Scott Corley ruled that the injunction covers contracts tied to sports including football, basketball, baseball, soccer, tennis, and Ultimate Fighting Championship events.

The tribes — Blue Lake Rancheria and the Chicken Ranch Rancheria of Me-Wuk Indians — must provide boundary maps by Nov. 13, the judge said.

Kalshi is required to report the status of the steps it has taken to comply with the injunction within 21 days, according to the order.

Sports and gaming attorney Daniel Wallach described the order as the first preliminary injunction secured by tribes against Kalshi nationwide, calling it "a major development in tribal gaming litigation."

The tribes filed the lawsuit in July 2025, alleging that Kalshi's sports event contracts constitute unauthorized Class III gaming under the Indian Gaming Regulatory Act (IGRA) and violate the tribes' gaming ordinances.

Kalshi has argued that its sports event contracts are federally regulated swaps under the Commodity Exchange Act (CEA), and that the CEA's grant of exclusive jurisdiction to the Commodity Futures Trading Commission preempts tribal gaming law.

The judge initially denied the tribes' motion for a preliminary injunction in November 2025. The tribes then appealed, and a Ninth Circuit panel partially reversed the decision in September.

"Reversing the district court's denial of a preliminary injunction in part, the panel held that the tribes were likely to succeed in their claims that Kalshi's sports event contracts on tribal lands violated IGRA and the tribes' gaming ordinances," the panel wrote.

The Ninth Circuit panel also held that the CEA's "exclusive" jurisdiction did not extend to Kalshi's sports event contracts.

The Block has reached out to Kalshi for comment.

Kalshi faces mounting legal challenges, with more states taking action against the prediction markets platform.

On Wednesday, Arkansas Attorney General Tim Griffin sued Kalshi for allegedly operating an illegal gambling platform in the state.

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said, adding that such practice is "squarely prohibited" by Arkansas law.

Last week, Utah also filed criminal charges against Kalshi and two company executives, accusing them of illegally promoting online gambling. Utah has also sued Kalshi and two Susquehanna trading firms for allegedly operating an illegal gambling business.

An editor checks the facts, figures and quotes they produce against primary sources, and every piece is reviewed by an editor before it is published. Sponsored content and press releases are labeled as such and are not written by our editorial team.

“Tapestry” was especially popular with GPT-4o. More recent AI models are more likely to use “quietly,” “leverage,” “challenge,” and “multifaceted.”

EV drivers spend roughly twice as much time with podcasts as other drivers, as well as more time with satellite radio and streaming music services.

One single word which proves the Nuno era is truly dead and buried | West Ham News

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Many fans feel they saw the very best, and the worst of West Ham United in the ‘game of two halves’ that unfolded before our eyes on Wednesday night.

Cardiff City’s threadbare, increasingly tiring squad no doubt helped the Hammers along the way, but the starting mindset seemed to sum up everything that was wrong with Nuno Espírito Santo’s West Ham  performances of late, and the second half recovery hopefully signposts what is to come under a less cautious Scott Parker.

One word stands out amongst all of the media interviews and comments made by the newly appointed head coach and his players after the game, which differentiates vividly the two managers’ styles. And hopefully points towards a fundamental shift now that Nuno is no longer.

High risk football: Nuno wasn’t capable of taking off the hand brake

As reported in the standard.co.uk, Scott Parker explained what he’d addressed at half time in the Cardiff dressing room.:

“Certainly out of possession, we needed to be more aggressive.“

There were tactical changes and substitutions, and we became more front-footed.

If any one word sums up the failure of the Nuno era it is that word: Risk. The cautious Portuguese manager cost the Hammers their Premier League place by trying to defend leads, throwing on defensive substitutes in frequent, vain hopes of keeping hold of a lead – and far too often set the team up to hang on rather than to score more.

And it was his insistence on doing the same this season, rather than setting out to dominate the division, that was driving home supporters to distraction and in the end cost him his job.

Whatever else, if only from from the first, brief but encouraging impressions of one half of football and from his words after, Scott Parker seems more prepared for the higher-risk football that goes with winning and dominating a campaign in this division.

His arrival was heralded by the moaners as the landing of ‘another negative coach’: Its of course early days, but that might prove to be a long way from the truth.

Ro Launches Research Lab to Turn Patient Data Into Clinical Evidence

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Ro announced the launch of Ro Research Lab on November 6, 2026, an initiative combining AI, real-world patient data and scientific research. Ro plans to publish early findings, submit those that hold up for peer review, and turn the evidence into clinical guidance, tools and care for patients within and outside its own platform.

The initiative targets the delay between a medical insight and an intervention that can be delivered at scale. It addresses a system in which patient information is dispersed, care outcomes are difficult to quantify and research can take years to influence treatment. Ro’s platform brings together clinical care, pharmacy, laboratory results and patient-reported outcomes, generating information on side effects, sleep, heart rate and weight across what the company describes as millions of patient journeys.

The research questions concern why patients respond differently to the same treatment. Ro wants to know why one person might lose 35% of their body weight on a medication while another loses 5%, why side effects vary, and how genetics, behavior and treatment decisions shape outcomes. AI will look for patterns, while scientific testing will be conducted alongside clinicians and academic researchers. Planned subjects include heart attacks, food noise, mental health and grocery purchases.

The lab’s publications catalogue includes an abstract evaluating an adverse-event triage tool in telehealth obesity care. Its custom large language model (LLM) detected patient messages about current or recent adverse events during GLP-1 treatment, classified their severity and generated high-priority alerts for licensed care-team review. The study table dates the work to November 30, 2024, through December 9, 2024, so it predates the formal launch.

Clinical pharmacists helped develop the structured severity information incorporated into the prompt. Human reviewers labeled annotated messages to assess detection precision, while the operational evaluation used a random sample of 3,500 messages sent in the four days before and five days after implementation. The model was already classifying messages before full deployment, but its outputs were not yet connected to routing or alert generation. That created a comparison of care-team response times before and after alerts were introduced.

The authors reported a 94% true-positive rate for detecting adverse-event content. Across 1,628 pre-implementation and 1,872 post-implementation messages, the median time to first clinical response fell from 115.1 minutes to 33.0 minutes, with a median reduction of 82.1 minutes (P <.001). Urgent adverse events after implementation had a median response time of 25.8 minutes.

The lab had also begun publishing before its formal announcement. An exercise analysis and the GLP-1 Pipeline Tracker introduction are both dated October 29, 2026. Together, they show two different forms of output: analysis of Ro patient experiences and a public tool organizing information about obesity-drug development.

Ro reported early weight loss of 5.03% among patients who reported no cardio and 5.24% among those reporting at least 2.5 hours per week. Patients reporting more exercise were also more likely to report increased energy, improved mood, better sleep and fewer cravings at month one, making the broader treatment experience part of the analysis rather than focusing exclusively on weight.

Exercise was self-reported at the first follow-up visit, approximately two weeks after treatment began. Researchers used linear regression to assess the association between activity and weight loss around month two, adjusting for baseline body mass index, age and biological sex. Ro cautioned that exercise was measured at only one time point, activity could change during follow-up, unmeasured confounding could influence results, and observational associations do not establish causation.

The pipeline tracker uses publicly available press releases, published papers and ClinicalTrials.gov records rather than Ro’s patient data. It provides searches by company, drug or drug type, a clinical-development timeline, efficacy readouts, participant demographics, adverse-event information and side-by-side trial views. Ro cautions that different trials are not directly comparable, even when their results are displayed together.

The common thread is a division of labor: AI detects patterns or flags messages, researchers test associations, and providers retain clinical decisions in the triage example. Ro’s broader ambition now depends on whether that division can turn continuous patient observations into peer-reviewed evidence and better guidance, rather than stopping at early findings.

Physicl Raises $10M Seed to Address Physical AI’s Edge-Case Problem

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Physicl announced on November 6, 2026, that it had raised $10 million in seed funding led by Inovia Capital, with participation from US Venture Partners, and launched Studio, its first application for generating synthetic training data for physical AI.

The round funds the company’s team in Paris and the San Francisco Bay Area and the platform behind Studio. Inovia partner Steve Woods described Physicl’s scalable, high-quality physical data grounded in real-world physics as a critical part of translating model capabilities into real-world performance.

Physicl is targeting rare conditions that can determine whether robots, autonomous systems and world models operate reliably outside demonstrations, including a cup partly hidden by a bag, a shadow crossing a counter or a box stacked slightly out of position. These cases are difficult to capture at scale and, once recorded, cannot be replayed with selected conditions changed.

Physicl argues that physical AI lacks a training corpus comparable to the internet text behind language models or the billions of captioned photos behind image models, leaving teams short of examples for the failures that can block deployment.

Founder and CEO Alex de Vigan has spent ten years digitizing objects and rooms in 3D, including building libraries and variation software for large retailers. Robotics, world-model and vision teams subsequently began requesting precisely labeled versions of situations their models failed to handle, in volumes of thousands. That demand provided the basis for Physicl’s synthetic-data platform.

Physicl has roughly 30 team members across 3D, simulation and machine learning, with offices in New York, San Francisco and Paris. Most spent six years building 3D production systems with de Vigan before he founded the company.

A product announcement published alongside the funding news describes a four-step workflow. A researcher describes a failure in plain language; Studio proposes a matching scene and variations; the researcher adjusts their ranges and fixes conditions such as the camera path; and the system renders and exports the result while recording the conditions behind each frame.

Studio draws on a library of 10,000 environments and 100,000 underlying assets built from real 3D geometry and known materials. Lighting, time of day, clutter, objects, materials, camera height and camera path can be changed independently or together. That permits controlled comparisons in which only selected conditions differ between clips.

Depth, surface normals, instance and class segmentation and two-dimensional boxes come from the same 3D scene that produces the image, rather than being predicted from the rendered picture or drawn by annotators. Exports include RGB images or video at resolutions up to 1920 by 1080, with per-frame camera pose and scene metadata covering lighting, layout and objects.

Studio is in beta and free during that period, with access opening in waves to laboratories, universities and companies developing vision, world-model and robotics models. Real-world data remains essential to the approach: Studio is designed to supplement it with rare cases that real capture cannot supply repeatedly and at volume. New scenes are added daily, and Physicl plans to develop the controls, ground-truth layers and export formats through feedback from research users.

Physicl reported that world-model company SpAItial more than halved its reconstruction error on real rooms after training with Physicl data. The evaluation came from Matthias Niessner’s team at TUM. Niessner is SpAItial’s CEO and a TUM professor, and is also listed as a Physicl advisor.

Studio’s beta puts Physicl’s premise to a wider test: whether repeatable, exactly labeled synthetic edge cases can improve models reliably enough for deployment in physical environments.

FA and WSL Football launch major study into ACL injuries in women’s football

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

To help deliver timely coverage of women's football, we may use AI tools to support content creation and editing. While we strive for accuracy and editorial quality, readers should independently verify important information.

The Football Association and WSL Football have teamed up with Imperial College London on a new study into ACL injuries, the knee problem that has cost the women’s game some of its biggest names in recent seasons. All clubs across the Barclays WSL and WSL2 are being asked to take part, from the players themselves to the medical, performance and coaching staff around them.

Announced on Thursday, the project is being run as a PhD programme funded by the FA. Imperial researcher Hanna Lehtovirta, based in the university’s Department of Mechanical Engineering, is leading the work, with Fortius Clinic knee surgeon Simon Ball also involved. According to the FA’s announcement, the aim is to find out why anterior cruciate ligament ruptures keep happening in the elite women’s game and to turn those findings into practical prevention advice.

The research is split into two stages. In the first, players complete surveys covering their injury record, how much they train and play, and hormonal, nutritional and psychological factors. Anyone who has ruptured an ACL since the 2018-19 campaign will have their answers set against those of players who have avoided the injury, in the hope of spotting patterns linked to risk.

The second stage goes back to the same group for interviews, and this time staff are brought in too. The FA says plenty of earlier research has looked at single factors, often in a lab, and that far less is known about how they combine in the daily routine of a professional squad. Lehtovirta said her team would be looking at everything “from minutes played and injury prevention practices at clubs to how players developed before they entered professional football.”

ESPN reports that data gathering began in June and that the study is set to run for three years.

The numbers explain the urgency. Research cited by ESPN suggests women are around twice as likely as men to suffer an ACL injury, and roughly two in every three cases happen with no contact from another player. England captain Leah Williamson and Beth Mead both missed the 2023 World Cup after rupturing the ligament, and Vivianne Miedema has also had to come back from the injury during her time in England.

Ritan Mehta, the FA’s Head of Women’s Medical, was clear about the scale of the problem. “ACL injuries remain one of the biggest challenges facing female footballers,” she said. “While significant progress has been made in understanding the factors that may contribute to these injuries, there is still much more to learn.”

WSL Football’s Medical Governance Lead, Dr Abosede Ajayi, pointed to the mix of football, healthcare and academia behind the project, saying the findings could “inform medical standards across the women’s game”. Ball put it more bluntly: “ACL injury is devastating for professional athletes,” he said, adding that “prevention is better than cure!”

For more on how ACL injuries have hit the women’s game, read our reports Spurs confirm record signing Alice Sombath has ruptured her ACL and Project ACL puts WSL injury crisis back in focus with cross-league research.

The English study follows a similar move in the United States, where the NWSL and its players’ association joined Project ACL in April. The FA also marked the launch on social media:

Together with the @BarclaysWSL, we have launched a new research project to better understand ACL injuries in women’s football.

Treasury, IRS finalize new Opportunity Zone designations

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Treasury and the IRS designated 6,576 census tracts as new Qualified Opportunity Zones under the Working Families Tax Cuts, effective Jan. 1, 2027, with a 10-year term and enhanced incentives for rural areas.  AI Summary

The U.S. Department of the Treasury and the Internal Revenue Service (IRS) designated 6,576 census tracts across all 50 states, territories and the District of Columbia as new Qualified Opportunity Zones, according to a Thursday announcement.

The action, which will take effect Jan. 1, 2027, will permanently renew the federal Opportunity Zone tax incentive and add enhanced benefits for investment in eligible rural communities. It also establishes a process to redesignate opportunity zones every 10 years, meaning that the newly announced map will remain in place through the end of 2036.

IRS Chief Executive Officer Frank J. Bisignano, in a press release, said that the permanent extension and expansion of the program will “give states an opportunity to attract long-term investment into underserved, rural and economically distressed areas”, and that “the IRS and Treasury are enabling targeted investment and growth in communities where it is needed the most.”

Opportunity Zones, initially created as part of the Tax Cuts and Jobs Act of 2017, are designed to incentivize investment in economically disadvantaged communities by allowing investors, including builders and developers, to defer or reduce capital gains taxes when they invest in qualified projects.

For housing developers and homebuilders, the updated map and permanent extension of the incentive should provide more clarity on eligible locations and long-term planning for the next decade.

That certainty stems from changes made by the Working Families Tax Cuts, which President Trump signed into law in 2025.

The legislation made the Opportunity Zone program permanent, established enhanced tax incentives for rural communities and created a process to redesignate eligible census tracts every 10 years.

In an announcement, Treasury said that the Working Families Tax Cuts build on more than $110 billion in private-sector investment made in Opportunity Zones since the program was launched in 2017. The bill narrowed the pool of eligible zones from 8,764 and strengthened incentives for rural communities. Although urban areas will continue to represent a significant share of designated zones, the changes could encourage more investment in rural markets with limited access to housing capital.The changes complement a provision in the 21st Century ROAD to Housing Act that allows the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD) to favor grant applications if their housing development is located in or primarily serves a designated Opportunity Zone. The aim of that provision is to spur more housing supply in economically distressed communities.

The program, which applies to both market-rate and below-market housing, is popular among residential developers. A 2025 Urban Institute analysis found that 64% of Opportunity Zone capital in Ohio went toward residential development, for example. And a separate 2025 analysis by the Economic Innovation Group concluded that the program roughly doubled housing supply in eligible zones.

Why Tulip Fire Can Ruin Your Spring Flowers – And the 6 Simple Planting Steps that Help You Prevent It

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Beautiful plants and ideas to brighten your garden.

Plant These 7 Flower Seeds in November for Gorgeous Early Blooms Next Year

Sorry Squirrels, This Simple Trick Stops Pests Eating Tulip Bulbs (and it Beats Chicken Wire Every Time)

What Your Dahlias Need Most After the First Frost Hits – 5 Ways to Save Your Favorite Blooms for Next Year

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Tulip fire can make your eagerly anticipated spring bulbs look distorted and spotty. But these quick bulb planting checks can help you to prevent calamity later

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Planting some tulips is one of the most enjoyable ways to pass some time this month. It offers the promise of vivid, saturated color to punctuate the winter months. Too often, however, the anticipation can turn to heartbreak, as early leaves emerge stunted, twisted, and scarred with spots. This is classic tulip fire: an aggressive fungal blight that leaves tulips looking as though they were scorched by an open flame.

The frustrating reality of tulip fire is that while symptoms appear in spring, the infection usually begins months earlier during tulip planting. The pathogen enters unnoticed on dry bulb scales, or lies dormant in beds where infected tulips grew before. Once established, no post-planting drench or spray can save infected stems. Your best defense is prevention before your tulip bulbs go in the ground. A few careful checks at planting time give next year's tulips a much better shot. Just a few proactive measures this fall will ensure that your tulips emerge clean, robust, and full of vibrant blooms. Here’s how to address the perils of tulip fire before it ever has a chance to cause problems – so you can look forward to spring with confidence.

Tulip fire is a common tulip disease caused by the fungal pathogen Botrytis tulipae. While closely related to common gray mold (B. cinerea), which attacks soft fruits and perennials, B. tulipae is host-specific and targets tulips almost exclusively. This host specificity is an advantage for gardeners. If you deny the fungus its preferred host for 3-4 seasons, resting populations in the soil gradually starve and decline.

Recognizing early symptoms is key to containment. In early spring, infected bulbs send up stunted, crumpled shoots known as fireheads. These sickly stems become blanketed in fuzzy gray fungal spores that blow or splash onto neighboring plants during cool, rainy weather. Tiny, water-soaked flecks quickly spread across foliage, coalescing into scorched brown blotches, while flower buds rot before opening. Wet spring conditions across USDA hardiness zones 3-8 accelerate spore dispersal, and can turn an isolated infection into an outbreak within a week.

Most cultivated tulip hybrids are at risk in damp, overcrowded conditions. However, species tulips with lower profiles (like Tulipa clusiana or T. tarda) and sturdy Darwin hybrid varieties demonstrate better resilience than dense, double-flowered late types. If you’re still deciding which bulbs to get, try bold Red Darwin Hybrid Tulip Bulbs from Amazon for weather resistant stems, and dreamily elegant Salmon Impression Darwin Hybrid Tulip Bulbs from Plant Addicts for natural vigor. Here's how to prevent your tulips turning into fireheads. Grab your bulbs and get ready!

Preventing tulip fire adds only a few minutes to your fall planting routine, but the benefits are immeasurable. Proactive defense focuses on basic cultural care: thoughtful checks, site and timing prep, and ample airflow. The following simple checks take just a little extra preparation, providing peace of mind and protecting your spring displays long before the first green shoots pierce the soil…

Before planting your bulbs, empty your bulb bags onto an outdoor potting bench or table, and inspect each one individually. A healthy, viable tulip bulb should feel solid, firm, and dense, with an intact papery outer tunic. Gently press the base plate and tip with your thumb. Any that give under your thumb, show mold, feel spongy, or have sunken yellow-brown patches should go in the trash.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Next, peel back a section of the papery tunic. Look carefully for sclerotia: tiny, hard, black or dark-brown resting bodies that resemble crushed pepper or poppy seeds. To help get a better look up close, the Jarlink 30X 60X Illuminated Jewelers Loupe, available from Amazon is equipped with bright LED illumination to check for those black sclerotia specks, as well as hidden scale lesions. These small sclerotia carry the tulip fire fungus between seasons. If you detect black specks or lesions, bag the bulb and throw it in the trash. Never toss diseased stock into home compost piles.

Resist the urge to plant your tulips while the ground is still warm. Botrytis tulipae fungal spores and resting sclerotia thrive in mild, damp soil conditions between 50°F and 65°F (10-18°C). Planting prematurely exposes bulbs to active soil-borne pathogens before the bulbs can establish robust roots. Delay your tulip planting until mid-to-late November or early December across most regions, after several hard overnight frosts have chilled the ground.

Soil temperatures should consistently measure below 50°F (10°C) at 6 inches (15cm). Cold soil suppresses fungal activity and forces dormant spores into inactivity. Planting after the first hard frosts is one of the most effective defenses against tulip fire. Even if an early frost hardens the surface, the soil beneath remains workable. Using a long-handled bulb planter like the Jardineer Bulb Transplanter from The Home Depot works well even in frost-encrusted fall soils.

Soil hygiene and thoughtful rotation are critical defenses against tulip fire. If your border suffered from tulip fire last spring, millions of sclerotia will have been shed into the earth as the leaves and flower petals decomposed. These resting bodies can survive in loam for 3-5 years, waiting to infect any new tulips that push up through the contaminated soil, and start the cycle all over again. Give that bed at least 3 years without tulips, and plant new bulbs somewhere that's never had the disease. Fill affected borders with non-host species that tulip fire cannot infect, such as daffodils (narcissus), alliums, grape hyacinths (muscari), or crocuses.

If bed space is tight, containers are an easy workaround. Transition your tulips into patio containers. However, always dump out old potting soil, sanitize containers with a dilute vinegar or mild bleach wash, and plant in fresh, sterile soilless potting mix enriched with perlite. A lovely blend is Espoma Organic Potting Mix, available from Walmart, which is free of garden pathogens, and gives a low-release nutrient boost.

A bulb that sits in soggy ground all winter is in trouble, whether tulip fire shows up or not. Good drainage is critical. Tulip bulbs thrive in moisture, but can rot quickly in stagnant water. Planting in heavy, compacted clay or low-lying depressions also creates damp conditions where fungal spores multiply rapidly. Check your planting site a day or so after a soaking rain. Water that is still standing there, or dirt that balls up and sticks to your boots, means the spot would drain too slowly for tulips.

Select a planting site situated on high ground or a gentle slope. If you garden on heavy clay, avoid digging individual narrow holes. Instead, excavate the whole planting area 8-10 inches (20-30cm) deep. Blend some horticultural grit or coarse sand into the soil to help with drainage. Alternatively, for persistently soggy gardens, skip the fight and grow your tulips in containers or raised beds. You can get small bags of best-selling Miracle-Gro Perlite from The Home Depot for just a few dollars, and sprinkle a few handfuls into the planting site (or use it in your containers!).

Crowded planting designs may look lush in magazine spreads, but tightly packed bulbs create stagnant, humid conditions. After a few years, offsets and daughter bulbs fill in a bit more each season, and after a while the leaves will crowd each other. If tulips are jammed together, leaves that touch will stay wet longer after rain or a heavy dew, and spores won't have far to go to get from one plant to the next.

Give your tulips room to breathe by spacing bulbs 4-6 inches (10-15cm) apart in all directions. Make sure the planting site is not jammed against a fence or the side of the house. Choose open, sunny sites with steady airflow. Use jute twine like Vivifying Natural Jute from Amazon looped around some wooden stakes to help mark your plantings. Also, if you are naturalizing tulips in mixed borders, lift and divide dense perennial clumps every few years before planting your bulbs.

Old tulip leaves don't just vanish when they're left on the soil. On plants that were sick, fresh sclerotia form on the dying tissue and drop into the bed, and they can stay there until tulips come up through that ground again. Infected leaves, decaying stem bases, and dropped flower petals all produce fresh sclerotia as they wither. Left untouched, it decomposes into the topsoil, recharging the fungal spore bank.

Before planting new bulbs, do a fall cleanup. Rake through your beds and clear out old leaves, spent stems, and decayed organic matter. Pull back mulch to inspect the soil, discarding any dried plant remnants and flower parts from the previous growing season. Make sure you bag these remnants instead of composting them. You can buy best-selling Fabulas Yard Waste Bags from Walmart in triple packs.

In spring, you need to stay proactive. Deadhead any spent flowers off suspect plants before their petals drop onto lower foliage, and clear out leaves and stems from anything that shows symptoms. Dig up any twisted firehead shoots, including the bulbs and adjacent soil. Do this the moment symptoms appear to keep airborne spores from spreading. Cleanup won't necessarily promise a clean bed, but it ensures there's a lot less fungus left behind to start the next round.

Once tulip fire takes hold in an established bed, it is one of the most persistent and frustrating bulb diseases to eradicate. So this fall care routine is massively beneficial in helping to break the fungal life cycle before it can cause damage. Take the right steps now when planting, and then you just have to wait for those first green shoots and a vibrant, spotless tulip display in spring.

Need more ideas for getting the most from your plants, indoors and out, and looking for the best seasonal expert advice delivered straight to your inbox? Sign up for the free Gardening Know How Newsletter!

Tyler’s passion began with indoor gardening and deepened as he studied plant-fungi interactions in controlled settings. With a microbiology background focused on fungi, he’s spent over a decade solving tough and intricate gardening problems. After spinal injuries and brain surgery, Tyler’s approach to gardening changed. It became less about the hobby and more about recovery and adapting to physical limits. His growing success shows that disability doesn’t have to stop you from your goals.

Core Scientific plans to shut down Bitcoin mining despite Q3 revenue hitting $233M

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Core Scientific, once the largest Bitcoin [BTC] miner, has released its Q3 2026 report, but at the same time it has also announced its plans to completely exit Bitcoin mining by the end of 2026.

During its Q3 2026 earnings call, Chief Financial Officer Jim Nygaard said the company expects to complete the mining wind-down in the fourth quarter and enter 2027 as a high-density colocation provider.

This decision came as the firm’s Q3 results were primarily driven by the expansion of its high-density colocation (HDC) business, which supports AI and high-performance computing (HPC) workloads.

Remarking on the same, CEO Adam Sullivan said,

We are nearing full delivery under our first colocation agreement while construction under our second is underway across five campuses.

According to the reports, the company’s colocation revenue surged nearly 60% quarter-over-quarter to $215.4 million, compared to $136.7 million in Q2 2026 and $15 million in Q3 2025.

In fact, its total revenue also increased to $232.9 million, up from $164.2 million in the previous quarter and $81.1 million a year earlier.

As of the 30th of September, Core Scientific was billing for 485 megawatts (MW) of capacity, up from 395 MW in the previous quarter, representing more than $700 million in average annualized colocation revenue.

That said, the company also reported net income of $1.178 billion, compared to a net loss of $1.15 billion in Q2 2026 and $144 million in Q3 2025.

Meanwhile, adjusted EBITDA more than tripled quarter-over-quarter to $133.6 million, with its margin expanding to 57% from 25% in the previous quarter. Core Scientific’s gross profit also climbed to $123.3 million, compared to $70 million in Q2.

However, on the mining side, self-mining revenue declined to $12.3 million from $21.5 million in the previous quarter, while hosted mining revenue stood at $5.17 million. At the same time, revenue from hosting equipment for other Bitcoin miners also declined to $5.2 million from $8.7 million.

For those unaware, Core Scientific began its AI infrastructure transition in 2024 through hosting agreements with CoreWeave, covering 590 MW of capacity, of which 485 MW was billable by September 2026. However, the transition also comes with challenges.

Core Scientific recorded $266.5 million in mining-related asset impairments in Q1 2026 amid weaker Bitcoin prices, low mining profitability, and declining equipment values. It also recorded a $41.9 million loss after terminating mining equipment agreements with Block and Proto in Q2.

That said, Core Scientific is not the only one, as, according to AMBCrypto’s recent report, Bitcoin miners are increasingly selling BTC to fund operations while shifting their infrastructure towards AI and HPC.

This comes as miners swung from a huge inflow spike of about +7K BTC  in early 2026 to an outflow of about -5.7K BTC in early November, while the price slid from around $95K to $75.5K.

However, the latest netflow has settled back to a small positive reading of about 179.4 BTC, which suggests the miner selling pressure has cooled.
